# History of Saint Pachomius

**Author**: Recueil de monographies  
**Series**: PO Tome 4  
**Columns**: pp. 424–504  

[p. 424] {#po.4.p424}

### LIFE OF THE BLESSED PACHOMIUS
ΒΙΟΣ ΤΟΥ͂ ΜΑΚΑΡΙΟΥ͂ ΠΑΧΟΥΜΙΟΥ͂". Ταθέννησίς" ἐστιν ἐν τῇ Θηβαΐδι, οὕτω καλουμένη, ἐν ἦ Παχούμιος ἐκ νέας ἡλικίας ἡ, τὸν μονήρη βίον ἐπιποθήσας, καταλαδὼν τὸν προλεχθέντα τόπον, γέγονεν τῷ χρόνῳ προ- κόπτων ἐφάμιλλος τῶν εὐσεθῶς βεδιωχότων. Οὗτος κατηξιώθη χαρίσματος προρρήσεων ' ναὶ ὀπτασίας ἀγγελικῆς, οὗτος εἰς ἄγαν ἐγένετο φιλάνθρωπος καὶ φιλάδελφος. Καθεζομένου ἡ οὖν αὐτοῦ ἐν τῷ σπηλαίῳ, ὥφθη αὐτῷ ἄγγελος Κυρίου, καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ" « Παχούμιε", τὰ κατὰ σαυτὸν χατορθώσας, περιττῶς οὖν χάθη" ἐν τῷ σπηλαίῳ. Δεῦρο οὖν ἐξελθὼν '" συνάγαγε πάντας τοὺς νεωτέρους μονάζοντας, καὶ οἴκησον μετ᾽ αὐτῶν,
[The Rule of Saint Pachomius] - 1. Tabennisi, so named, is in the Thebaid. Pachomius, who desired the monastic life from his youth, arrived at the aforementioned place when he was already advanced in age and became the emulator of those who had lived in piety. He was graced with the gift of prophecy and angelic vision; he became a great friend of men and of (his) brothers. 2. When he was dwelling in a cave, an angel of the Lord appeared to him and said: "Pachomius, now that you have put your personal affairs in order, you have remained too long in a cave. Go therefore and gather

καὶ κατὰ τὸν τύπον τὸν διδόμενόν σο! οὕτως αὐτοῖς νομοθέτησον. » Καὶ ἐπιδέδωχεν αὐτῷ δέλτον χαλκῆν, ἐν ἦ ἐγέγραπτο ' οὕτως"
all the young monks, dwell with them, and bring them laws according to the model that is given to you"; and he gave him a tablet of brass on which it was written:
« Συγχωρήσεις" ἑκάστῳ κατὰ δύναμιν, καὶ φαγεῖν καὶ πιεῖν, χαὶ πρὸς ᾿ τὰς δυνάμεις τῶν ἐσθιόντων ἀνάλογα ' καὶ τὰ ἔργα αὐτοῖς ἐγχείριζε", χαὶ μήτε νηστεῦσαι χωλύσης μήτε φαγεῖν. Ποίησον δὲ κέλλας διαφόρους ἐν τῇ αὐλῇ, καὶ τρεῖς κατὰ κέλλαν μενέτωσαν. Ἢ δὲ τροφὴ πάντων ὑπὸ ἔνα οἰκονόμον ἐξεταζέσθω. ἸΚαθευδέτωσαν δὲ ἐν ταῖς κέλλαις μὴ ἀνακείμενοι' ὁ ἀλλὸ θρόνους οἰκοδομητοὺς, ὑπτιωτέρους, ἐν ταῖς κέλλαις ποίησον, ἵνα ἐν τῷ χαθεύδειν ἐν αὐτῷ χαὶ χάθηνται"". Φορείτωσαν δὲ ἐν ταῖς νυξὶν λεθιτῶνας "ὁ λινοῦς καὶ ἐζωσμένοι, ἕκαστος δὲ αὐτῶν ἐχέτω μηλωτὴν αἰγίαν εἰργασμένην, ἧς ἄνευ μηδὲ ἐσθιότωσαν '", μηδὲ καθουδέτωσαν: Εἰσιόντες δὲ εἰς τὴν χοινωνίαν " χατὰ σάδδατον καὶ κυριακὴν, τὰς ζώνας λυέτωσαν, καὶ τὴν μιηλωτὴν ἀποτιθέσθωσαν, καὶ μετὰ χουχουλλίου μόνου '" εἰσίτωσαν. » ἹΚουχούλλια δὲ αὐτοῖς
3. "Dispense to each, as you are able, food and drink and * impose upon them tasks proportioned to the strength of those who eat; do not prevent either fasting or eating. However, you shall give the arduous tasks to the most robust and to those who eat; and the easy tasks to those who show the most asceticism and to those who devote themselves at home to long prayers. Divide the dwelling into various cells; let them dwell three in each of them. Let the food of all be prepared by a steward. Let them sleep in the cells without lying down, but make in the cells high and sloping seats, so that even for sleeping they may be seated. Let them wear at night linen tunics without sleeves and belts; let each of them have a melote (that is to say, a skin) of prepared goat, and let them not eat or sleep without it. To go to receive communion * on Saturday and Sunday, they will untie the belts and put down the melotes; they will approach with only the cuculla (with a hood)." He made them cucullae without hair like those for children and prescribed putting on them


[p. 427] {#po.4.p427}
ἐτύπωσεν ἄμαλλα ὡς παιδίοις, ἐν οἷς χαὶ κατηναρίδια πορφυρίου ἐκέλευσεν τίθεσθαι. ᾿Εχέ- λευσεν δὲ εἰχοσιτέσσαρα τάγματα εἶναι, καὶ ἑκάστῳ τάγματι πεποίηκεν στοιχεῖον ' “Ἕλληνι- κὸν, ἀπὸ τοῦ Ἄλφα, καὶ Βῆτα, καὶ Γάμμα, καὶ Δέλτα, καὶ χαθ᾽ ἑξῆς.
purple braids. He also prescribed forming twenty-four groups and he assigned to each group a Greek letter, from Alpha, Beta, Gamma, Delta, etc.
Ἐν τῷ οὖν ἐρωτᾶν καὶ πολυπραγμονεῖν τὸν Παχούμιον τὸν ἄγγελον' « Ἦ᾽ εἰς τοσοῦτον πλῆθος, πῶς ὁ πρῶτος ἐρωτήσει τὸν δεύτερον; » εἶπεν αὐτῷ" « Τοῖς ὀκεραιοτέροις ἢ καὶ ἁπλουστέροις, ἰδίῳ τινὶ σημείῳ καλέσουσιν, τὸ πῶς ἔχει τὸ γάμμα ", ὡσανεὶ τὸ τρίτον τάγμα, εἶτα πῶς ἔχει τὸ ζῆτα, ἔδδομον τάγμα, ἄσπασαι τὸ ῥῶ, καὶ " οὕτως ἑαυτοὺς ἐπι- σχεπτέσθωσαν. Τοῖς δὲ σχολιωτέροις ἐπιθήσεις" τὸ ξῖ. » Καὶ οὕτως κατὰ ἀναλογίαν τῆς καταστάσεως τῶν στοιχείων, ἁρμοζουσῶν τῶν προαιρέσεων καὶ τῶν βίων, ἑκάστῳ τάγματι στοιγεἶον ' ἐφήρμοσεν, μόνων τῶν πνευματικῶν εἰδότων τὰ σημαινόμενα. Ἐγέγραπτο δὲ χαὶ τοῦτο ἐν τῇ δέλτῳ ὅτι ξένον ἄλλου μοναστηρίου, ἄλλον ἔχοντα τύπον, οὐ δέξῃ, πλὴν τοῦ ξενοδοχεῖν, τούτοις μὴ συνφαγεῖν, μὴ συμπιεῖν, μὴ εἰσαγαγεῖν εἰς τὴν μονὴν ἐχτὸς εἰ μὴ εὑρεθείη γνωστός ", ἢ" θέλοντα συνεῖναι αὐτοῖς, τὸν τοιοῦτον ἐπὶ τριετίαν '᾽ εἴσω τῶν ἀδύτων μὴ εἰσαγαγεῖν, ἀλλ᾽ ἐργατικώτερα ἔργα ἐπιτελοῦντα, οὕτως εἰσδέχεσθαι μετὰ τριετίαν 'δ.
4. As Pachomius questioned the angel and inquired: "In such a great multitude, how will the archimandrite question (his) second?" he said to him: "They will designate the purest and the most ingenuous by an appropriate letter: how is the Gamma, for example, for the third group; then how is the Zeta, the seventh group; greet the Rho - * it is thus that they will inspect them. - To the most tortuous, you will attribute the Xi." Thus, by analogy with the shape of the letters and in accordance with conduct and life, he gave the suitable letter to each group. The spiritual ones alone knew the meaning. It was also written on the tablet: "You shall not permit a stranger from another monastery, having another way of life - in addition to hospitality - to eat and drink with (yours) nor to enter the monastery, unless he happens to be a relative. Likewise, you shall not (permit) one who wants to join them to enter inside the sanctuaries before three years, but when he has accomplished the most laborious tasks, one will receive him after three years."


[p. 428] {#po.4.p428}
«δ. ᾿Εσθιόντες ' δὲ ἐν ἑνὶ οἴκῳ τὰς χεφαλὰς χαλυπτέτωσαν τοῖς χουχουλλίοις, ἵνα " μὴ ἀδελφὸς ἀδελφὸν μασσώμενον᾽ ἴδει", οὐδὲ ἐξεῖναι λαλεῖν ἐσθίοντα ', οὐδὲ ἐκτὸς τοῦ πίνακος καὶ τῆς τραπέζης ἀλλαχοῦ προσέχειν τῷ ὀφθαλμῷ, διὰ πάσης δὲ ἡμέρας ποιεῖν εὐχὰς δώδεκα, ὡς εἶναι τὴν εὐχὴν ἄπαυστον, καὶ ἐν ταῖς παννυχίσιν ὡσαύτως, χαὶ ἐνάτην ὥραν, ὅτε δοχεῖ τὸ τὔληθος μεταλαμθάνειν, τρεῖς εὐχὰς, καὶ ἐν τῷ λυχνικῷ, ἐξ εὐχὰς, καὶ ἑκάστῃ εὐχῇ προσάδεσθαι ψαλμόν. »
5. Those who eat in the same hall will cover their heads with the cucullae, so that * a brother may not see a brother chewing. He who eats must not speak nor cast his eyes elsewhere, outside of the tablet and the table. Each day (it is necessary) to perform twelve prayers, so that the prayer may be incessant, and just as many during the night. At the ninth hour, when the great number deems it good to eat, (one will perform) three prayers, and in the evening, six prayers; at each prayer one will sing (a) psalm."
Προσαντιλέγοντος δὲ τοῦ Παχουμίου τῷ ἀγγέλῳ ὅτι ὀλίγαι εἰσὶν αἱ εὐχαὶ, λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ ἄγγελος" « Ἵκανόν. Ταύτας δὲ διετύπωσα, ὡς φθάνειν καὶ τοὺς μιχροὺς ἐπιτελεῖν τὸν᾽ κανόνα χαὶ μὴ λυπεῖσθαι" ὡς ἀποιήτους᾽ οἱ δὲ τέλειοι", νομοθεσίας χρείαν ᾽ οὐκ ἔχουσιν καθ᾽ ἑαυτοὺς γὰρ ἐν ταῖς χέλλαις" ὅλον ἑαυτῶν τὸ ζῆν τῇ τοῦ Θεοῦ νομοθεσίᾳ παραχωρεί- τωσαν".»
Pachomius pointing out to the angel that the prayers were few in number, the angel said to him: "That suffices; I have established these (prayers), so that even the little ones may arrive at accomplishing the rule and not be afflicted as if they were poorly fashioned. As for the perfect, they have no need of law for, withdrawn into their cells, they devote their entire existence to the divine law."
θ0. Ταῦτα νομοθετήσας ὁ ἀγγελὸς ἹΚυρίου τῷ Παχουμίῳ, χαὶ δοὺς αὐτῷ " τὴν δέλτον ἀπέστη ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ. Ἐποίησεν δὲ ὁ Παχούμιος ὅσα συνέταξεν αὐτῷ ἐν τῇ δέλτῳ ὁ ἀγγελὸς Κυρίου, καὶ συνεστήσατο μοναστήρια τρία, ἐν οἷς τὸ πρῶτον χαὶ μέγιστον μοναστήριον, ἔνθα αὐτὸς ὁ Παχούμιος κεῖτο, καὶ τὰ ἄλλα ἄποιχοι ἦσαν τὰ μοναστήρια, ἀνδρῶν χιλίων τριακοσίων, τὸ δὲ ἐφ᾽ ἑξῆς ἀνδρῶν διακοσίων, εἶτα τὸ μετ᾽ αὐτῶν, ἀνδρῶν τριακοσίων: ἐκ τούτων χαὶ ἄλλα πλεῖστα '" ἐγένοντο μοναστήρια κρατήσαντα τὸν αὐτὸν τύπον, συντείνοντα ' εἰς ἑπτακισχιλίους ἄνδρας.
6. The angel of the Lord gave these laws to Pachomius, handed him * the tablet, and went away. Pachomius did everything that the angel of the Lord had commanded him on the tablet and founded three monasteries, among which the first and the principal, where Pachomius himself dwelt and from which the other monasteries proceeded, contained one thousand three hundred men, the following one contained two hundred, and the one that followed them three hundred. From those proceeded the other numerous monasteries, attached to the same rule and containing up to seven thousand men.


[p. 429] {#po.4.p429}
Ἔν τούτῳ τῷ μοναστηρίῳ, ἐν ῳ ὁ Παχούμιος ᾧκει, γεγόνασιν δὲς τὸ μέτριον τῆς χώρας καὶ τὴν τῶν ἀδελφῶν πληθὺν, τέχναι διάφοροι, ῥάπται δεκαπέντε, χαλκεῖς ἑπτὰ, τέκτονες τέσσαρες, δεκαπέντε χναφεῖς, σκυτοτόμοι δεκαπέντε, βυρσεῖς εἴκοσι, κηπωροὶ εἴκοσι, ζεύγη βοῶν, χαὶ τούτων " οἱ ἀροτῆρες ' πεντήχονται, χαλλιγράφοι δέχα, χκαμιηλάριοι " δώδεκα, πλέκοντες σπυρίδας τὰς μεγάλας εἴχοσι, ἄλλοι πλέκοντες τὰ λεγόμενα μαλάκια τὰ σπυριδάλλια, ὡσαύτως εἴχοσ!, καὶ φύλαχας" δὲ τρέφουσιν δέχα. Οἱ οὖν ἐφημερευταὶ τοῦ μαγειρείου " ὄρθριοι ἀνιστάμενοι, οἱ μὲν αὐτῶν περὶ τὸ μαγειρεῖον ", οἱ δὲ περὶ τὰς τραπέζας γίνονται "- ἵστωσιν᾽ οὖν αὐτὰς ἀπαρτίσαντες ἀποτι- θέμενοι λαμψάνας, συνθετὰς ἐλαίας, διαφόρους τυροὺς καὶ ἄρτον, καὶ λεπτολάχανα. Οἱ οὖν ἐν τοῖς τοιούτοις ἔργοις συλλεγόμενοι, εἰσίχσιν ἕχοιστος οἰκείᾳ προθέσε: εἰς τὸ μεταλαβεῖν, οἱ μὲν ἕκτην ὥραν, οἱ δὲ ἑδδόμην, ἄλλοι ὀγδοήν, ἕτεροι δὲ ἐνάτην, ἄλλοι δεκάτην, ἄλλοι δὲ βαθεῖαν ἑσπέραν, καὶ οἱ μὲν ὑπὲρ μίαν, ἄλλοι " δὲ διὰ δύο, ἕχαστος χαθὼς βούλεται, χατὰ τὴν νομοθεσίαν τοῦ ἀγγέλου.
7. In this monastery where Pachomius lived, there were, according to the measure (resources) of the country and the number of the brothers, various craftsmen: fifteen tailors, seven blacksmiths, four carpenters, fifteen fullers, fifteen shoemakers, twenty tanners, twenty gardeners, pairs of oxen with their fifty plowmen, ten calligraphers, twelve camel drivers, twenty to weave large baskets, and also twenty to weave small baskets called Malakia. Finally, they maintain ten guards. Those who are on duty in the kitchen, rising early in the morning, busy themselves, some in the kitchen, others at the tables; they set the (tables), make preparations, place mustard (seeds), prepared olives, various cheeses, bread, and small vegetables. Those who are chosen for such tasks will go to eat each according to their own plan, some at the sixth hour, others at the seventh, others at the eighth, others at the ninth, others at the tenth, others late in the evening, and some every day, others every two days, each according to his will following the order of the angel.
Εἰσὶν δὲ χαὶ μοναστήρια γυναικῶν, τῶν Ταύεννησιωτῶν οὕτω καλούμενα, ὡς τετραχοσίων, τὴν αὐτὴν διατύπωσιν, τὴν αὐτὴν πολιτείαν τοῖς Ταδεννησιώταις ἔχοντες, ἐχτὸς τῆς μιηλωτῆς, αὗται δὲ πέραν τοῦ ποταμοῦ διάγουσιν. ᾿Ἑὰν οὖν τελευτήσῃ παρθένος,
8. There also exist monasteries for women, named the Tabennesiotes; they are four hundred in number and have the same rules and the same conduct as the monks of Tabennisi, with the exception of the melote; they live on the other side of the river. If, therefore, a virgin dies, the other virgins who

ἐνταφιάσασαι αὐτὴν αἱ λοιπαὶ παρθένοι ἄγουσιν καὶ τιθέασιν αὐτὴν παρὰ τὴν ὄγθαν τοῦ ποταμοῦ, Περῶντες δὲ οἱ ἀδελφοὶ, ἅμα τῷ πρεσδυτέρῳ καὶ τῷ διαχόνῳ, μετὰ πορθμείου ', κλάδους ἐλαιῶν καὶ βαείων ἐπιφερόμενοι, οὕτως μετὰ ψαλμῳδίας ἄγουσιν αὐτὴν εἰς τὸ πέραν, χαὶ ἐν τοῖς ἰδίοις " μνήμασιν ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ θάπτουσιν αὐτήν.
perform her burial lead her and place her near the riverbank. The brothers cross it on a ferry, with the priest and the deacon, carrying olive and palm branches; they lead her with psalmodies to the other side and bury her in the monastery, in their own tombs.
Ἐπαναδραμόντες δὲ τῷ λόγῳ, συγγενῇ τῶν προτέρων ἐροῦμεν Ἔθος ἦν τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς τοῦ ὁσίου πατρὸς Παχουμίου, ἐφ᾽ ἑκάστης ἡμέρας, εἰς ὡρισμένον τόπον τῆς μονῆς συνέρχεσθαι, ἐπὶ τῷ ἀχούειν τῆς διδασχαλίας αὐτοῦ. Συναχθέντων οὖν κατὰ ἔθος πάντων, κελεύει Θεοδώρῳ τινὶ ἀδελφῷ, εἰκοσαέτη χρόνον ἔχοντι" ἐν τῇ μονῇ, λαλῆσαι τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς τὰ πρὸς οἰκοδόμην καὶ ὠφέλειαν: ὃς παραχρῆμα, ἄνευ πάσης παραχοῆς, ἐλάλησεν αὐτοῖς τὰ πρὸς σωτηρίαν ψυχῆς συντείνοντα ὁ καθὸ εὐπόρησεν τῷ λόγῳ.
9 On Theodore - But, returning to our subject, we shall say a few things related to the preceding ones - The brothers of the pious and holy abbot Pachomius were accustomed to gather every evening in a designated place in the monastery to listen to his teaching. All being gathered as usual to hear the Great one, he commanded a certain Theodore, who had been in the monastery for twenty years, to speak to the brothers. "The latter immediately, without any disobedience, told them what was useful to them."
Τινὲς δὲ τῶν ἀρχαιοτέρων γερόντων ἰδόντες ᾿ ὅτι νεωτέρῳ ἐπέτρεψεν τοῦ λαλῆσαι οὐκ ἐβουλήθησαν ἀκοῦσαι αὐτοῦ, λέγοντες" « Εἰ ἤδη ἀρχάριος ἡμᾶς διδάσκει, οὐκ ἀκουσόμεθα αὐτοῦ. » " Καὶ καταλείψαντες τὴν σύναξιν τῶν ἀδελφῶν, ἀνεχώρησαν εἰς τὰ κελλία ἑαυτῶν. ᾿Απολυθέγτων δὲ τῶν ἀδελφῶν ἀπὸ τῆς ἀκροάσεως, ἀποστείλας ὁ μαχάριος Παχούμιος
10 Some of the oldest elders, seeing what was happening, did not want to listen to him; they said to themselves: "Since a beginner is instructing us, we will not listen to him," and they left the assembly of the brothers, departed from there, and went to their cells. When the brothers had left the assembly, the Great one had those who had withdrawn and did not want


[p. 431] {#po.4.p431}
μετεστείλατο τοὺς ἀναχωρήσαντας γέροντας, καὶ μὴ βουληθέντας ἀκοῦσαι τοῦ Θεοδώρου. ᾿Ελθόντων δὲ αὐτῶν ἐπηρώτησεν αὐτοὺς λέγων" « Τίνος ἕνεκα χαταλείψαντες ἡμᾶς, ἀνεχωρή- σατε εἰς τὰ κελλία ὑμῶν; » ΟἹ δέ φασιν ὅτι" « Παιδίον διδάσκαλον ἡμῶν ἐποίησας, τοσούτων γερόντων ἑστώτων, καὶ ἄλλων ἀρχαιοτέρων ἀδελφῶν ὄντων, παιδίῳ ἐχέλευσας διδάσκειν ἡμᾶς. ν
to hear Theodore called. When they came near the saint, he asked them: "Why did you leave us and return to your cells?" They said: "Because you gave us a child as a master; and in the presence of all the elders and the other older brothers, you ordered a child to speak to us."
᾿Ακούσας δὲ ταῦτα ὁ ὅσιος ἀνὴρ ἐστέναξεν εἰπών' « Ὦ, πόσου χρόνου καμάτους ἐν μιᾷ καιροῦ ῥοπῇ ἀπωλέσατε ! οὐχ οἴδατε! πόθεν ἡ ἀρχὴ τοῦ κακοῦ ἐχώρησεν εἰς τὸν κόσμον; » " Τῶν δὲ εἰρηχότων' « Πόθεν; » εἶπεν αὐτοῖς" « Ἔχ τῆς ὑπερηφανίας δὺ ἣν ἐξέπεσεν ὁ Ἔωσ- φόρος,ὁ πρωὶ ἀνατέλλων, καὶ συνετρίδη ἐπὶ τὴν γῆν᾽ δι᾽ ἣν καὶ μετὰ θηρίων συνῴκησεν Να- Θουχοδονοσὸρ ὁ βασιλεὺς Βαθυλῶνος. Ἢ οὐκ ἠκούσατε ' τῆς θείας γραφῆς λεγούσης" Βδέλυγμα ἐνώπιον κυρίου, πᾶς ὑψηλοκάρδιος; καί: Πᾶς ὁ ὑψῶν ἑαυτὸν ταπεινωθήσεται, ὁ δὲ ταπει- νῶν ἑαυτὸν ὑψωθήσεται. ᾿Εσκυλεύθητε " ὑπὸ τοῦ διαδόλου πάσης ὑμῶν ἀρετῆς, ἀγνοήσαντες" μητὴρ γὰρ πάντων τῶν χαχῶν καὶ ἀρχηγὸς ἡ ὑπερηφανία. Οὐ γὰρ Θεόδωρον καταλείποντες ἀπέλθατε, ἀχλὰ τὸν λόγον τοῦ Θεοῦ ἀποφυγόντες ἐξεπέσατε ἀπὸ τοῦ ἁγίου πνεύματος. ᾿Αλχηθῶς ἄθλιοι, καὶ πάντος οἴχτου ἄξιοι, "πῶς οὐκ ἐνοήσατε" ὅτι ὁ Σατανᾶς ἦν ὁ ἐνεργήσας ὑμῖν τοῦτο, οὗ χάριν ἀπεσχοινίσθητε᾽ τοῦ Θεοῦ, Ὦ μεγάλου θαύματος καὶ ἡμῶν κατακρίσεως, ὁ Θεὸς ἐταπείνωσεν ἑαυτὸν γενόμενος ὑπήκοος ἄγρι θανάτου δὺ ἡμᾶς, καὶ ἡμεῖς, Ὑὴ καὶ σποδὸς ὑπάρχοντες κατὰ φύσιν, ὑπερηφανευόμεθα. ᾿Ανετράπη παρ᾽ ἡμῶν
11 When the Great one heard this, he groaned and said: "Do you know how evil began in the world?" As they said "From where?" he answered them and said: "From pride, which caused the star Lucifer, who rises in the morning, to fall, and it shattered upon the earth." Have you not heard what is written: The man with a haughty heart is an abomination before the Lord and: Whoever exalts himself will be humbled, but he who humbles himself will be exalted. The demon has stripped you of all your virtue and you have not noticed it, for pride is the mother and the principle of all evils. It is not (only) Theodore that you abandoned by leaving, but when you fled the word of God, you lost the Holy Spirit. Wretched indeed and worthy of all compassion, how did you not understand that it was Satan who was acting in you? For this reason, therefore, you have distanced yourselves from God. O the great wonder! God humbled Himself and was obedient unto death


[p. 432] {#po.4.p432}
ἢ φύσις" ὁ πανύψηλος καὶ λίαν ὑπερμεγεθὴς, διὰ τῆς ταπεινότητος, τὸν κόσμον εἰς ἑαυτὸν πρὸς σωτηρίαν ἐσχγήνευσεν, δυνάμενος καὶ βλέμματι μόνον τὰ πάντα χαταφλέξαι.
for us, and we who are humble by nature, we pride ourselves. The order has been reversed by us: He who is exalted above all and who is great to excess, won the world through humility, when He could have consumed everything with a si-
Ἡμεῖς δὲ μηδὲν ὄντες φυσιούμεθα, ἀγνοοῦντες ὅτι ἐν τῷ τιμὴν ἡμᾶς ἐπιζητεῖν, μᾶλλον ἑαυτοὺς χκαταδικάζομεν εἰς τὰ χατώτατα τῆς γῆς. Οὐκ εἴδατε' ἐμὲ στήχοντα, χαὶ ἀκροώμενον τῆς διδασχαλίας “ αὐτοῦ; Ἔπ᾽ ἀληθείας λέγω, ὅτι ὠφελήθην μεγάλως ἀκούσας αὐτοῦ, οὐ γὰρ ὡς δοκιμάζων ἐπέτρεφα αὐτῷ λαλῆσαι ὑμῖν, ἀλλ᾽ εἰδὼς ὅτι δύναται ὁ Θεὸς χαὶ ἐν νεωτέρῳ δοῦναι λόγον ὠφελίας τοῖς ἀκούουσιν. ΤΥ γὰρ λέγει ὁ ἀπόστολος; ὅτι ἐὰν τῷ ἐσχάτῳ ἀπο- χαλυφθῇ, ὁ πρῶτος σιγάτω" ὡσανεὶ εὐχαριστῶ τῷ Θεῷ τῷ διδόντι" πᾶσιν ἁπλῶς καὶ μὴ ὀνειδίζοντι. Πόσῳ οὖν μᾶλλον ὑμᾶς ἔδει μετὰ πολλῆς τῆς προθυμίας καὶ ταπεινοφροσύνης ἀκοῦσαι αὐτοῦ; Πολλοὶ ἦσαν ἐν Αἰγύπτῳ παλαιότερο!: χαὶ σοφώτεροι κατὰ σάρχα τοῦ ἁγίου νέου" ἐν ἡλικίᾳ Ἰωσὴφ, ἀλλὰ διὰ τὴν ἐν αὐτῷ ἐπουράνιον σοφίαν, πάντων ὕπερ ἀναδέ- ὄηχεν, τῶν ἐν ὑπερηφανίχ χαταγηρασάντων. Ἔν ἀληθείᾳ λέγω ὑμῖν, ὅτι ἐγὼ ὁ πατὴρ ὑμῶν ἐν Κυρίῳ, ὡς μὴ εἰδὼς δεξιὰν ἢ ἀριστερὰν, οὕτως διὰ τὸν φόδον " τοῦ Θεοῦ ἐπη-- κροώμην αὐτοῦ ὠφεληθείς. ᾿Ἑὰν οὖν μὴ μεγάλην ὑπὲρ τοῦ σφάλματος τούτου ἐπιδείξεσθε
and we who are nothing, we pride ourselves without noticing that by this pursuit, we are sinking especially into the depths of the earth. Did you not see that I remained and listened to his teaching? In truth I tell you, I profited much from having listened to him. It is not to test him that I asked him to speak to you, but because I expected to derive profit from it myself. How much more reason did you have to listen to his word with good will and great humility! "In truth I am your father in the Lord; I listened to him with all my soul as if I were a man who does not know his right from his left. - I tell you therefore before God, if you do not show great repentance for this sin, to the point of weeping and groaning over yourselves, so that your fault may be forgiven you, if you do not do this, you will go to perdition."
πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν μετάνοιαν, ἵνα συγχωρηθῇ ὑμῖν τῆς ὑπεροψίας τὸ παράπτωμα, εἰ δὲ μή γε', εἰς ἀπώλειαν ἔσεσθε". » Ἦν ποτέ τις ἐν τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς, Σιλόανὸς ὀνόματι, εἰχοσαετὴ χρόνον ἔχων ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ, ἐν τῷ σχήματι τῷ μοναχικῷ" οὗτος δὲ ἦν ἀπὸ μίμων, ὃς ἐν ταῖς ἀρχαῖς
12 On Silvanus. - There was once among the brothers a man named Silvanus who had worn the monastic habit for twenty years. He was a mime. At


[p. 433] {#po.4.p433}
πάνυ ἐπεμελεῖτο' τῆς ἑαυτοῦ ψυχῆς. Χρόνου δὲ πολλοῦ παρελθόντος, ἤρξατο καταφρονεῖν τῆς ἑαυτοῦ σωτηρίας, ὥστε βούλεσθαι: αὐτὸν σπαταλᾶν, καὶ στρηνιᾶν λοιπὸν, ἔτι γε μὴν χαὶ τὰ τῆς θυμέλης ἄσεμνα ῥήματα ἀδεῶς μεταξὺ τῶν ἀδελφῶν τραγῳδεῖν ᾽. Τοῦτον χαλέσας ὁ μακάριος Παχούμιος ἐπὶ τῶν ἀδελφῶν, ἐχέλευσεν ἀποδυθῆναι τὸ σχῆμα τοῦ ἡ μοναχοῦ, καὶ λαθόντα αὐτὸν τὰ κοσμικὰ ἱμάτια ἐκδληθῆναι τῆς μονῆς ὑπὸ τῶν ἀδελφῶν. Ὁ δὲ πεσὼν ὑπὸ τοὺς πόδας αὐτοῦ παρεκάλει αὐτὸν, λέγων ὅτι" « ᾿Εὰν συγχωρήσῃς ἔτι τὸ ἄπαξ τοῦτο, ὦ πάτερ, τὸ υἡ ἐξῶσαί " με τῆς μονῆς, ἕξεις με ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν μετανοοῦντα
the beginning, he cared much for his soul, but shortly after, he began to neglect his salvation, to the point of wanting to act badly, to mock, and to sing without restraint among the brothers the light words that are said in the theater (thymele). The holy father Pachomius called him before the brothers and ordered him to take off the monastic habit, to put on secular clothes, and to leave the monastery and the brothers. He threw himself at his feet, begged him, and said to him: "If you forgive me this time again, O father, without throwing me out, you will see me from now on full of repentance for the things I was doing through negligence, to the point that you will rejoice at the chan-
ἐφ᾽ οἷς ἐν ἀμελείᾳ διῆγον, ὥστε σε χαρῆναι ἐπὶ τῇ μεταδολῇ τῆς ψυχῆς μου. » Ὃ δὲ ἅγιος ἀποκριθεὶς εἶπεν αὐτῷ" « Οἶδας πόσα σε ἐδάστασα, καὶ πόσα σε ἐνουθέτησα, ὥστε με καὶ τυπτῆσαί σε πολλάκις, ἄνθρωπον μὴ βουλόμενον μήδε" τὴν χεῖρα ἐχτεῖναί ἢ ποτε ἐπὶ τοιούτῳ, ἐφ᾽ οἷς νῦν ἀνάγκῃ πράττω ἐπὶ σοῦ. Ὃς πλέον σον τοῦ τυπτομένου, ἤλγουν τὴν ψυχὴν, τῷ τῆς συμπαθείας θεσμῷ᾽ διὰ γὰρ τὴν ἐν Θεῷ σου σωτηρίαν τύπτειν σε ἔδοξα, ἵν᾽ ἐκ τούτου δυνηθῶ διορθώσασθαί " σε τοῦ σφάλματος. Εἰ τοίνυν " νουθετούμενος οὐ μετετράπης, οὔτε παρα- καλούμενος ἐπὶ τὸ χρεῖττον οὐ μετηνέχθης ΄, οὔτε μὴν τυπτόμενος οὐκ ἐφοδήθης, πῶς δύνα- μαΐ σοι συγχωρῆσαι ἔτι; » ᾿Επεκτείνοντος δὲ τὴν παράκλησιν τοῦ Σιλδανοῦ, καὶ διαδεξαιου- μένου διορθοῦσθαι ἑαυτὸν τοῦ λοιποῦ, ἐγγύας ἤτησεν παρ᾽ αὐτοῦ ὁ ὅσιος πατὴρ, μὴ, μετὰ τὸ ἀφεθῆναι αὐτὸν, τοῖς αὐτοῖς ἐμμείνη " πάλιν. Πετρωνίου δέ τινος εὐλαδοῦς ἀδελφοῦ
The saint answered him and said to him: "You know how much I have endured you and how much I have reprimanded you; I even struck you often, I who am a man who does not want to raise his hand for such a thing as I had to do by necessity regarding you, especially because by striking you, my soul suffered out of sympathy. I struck you for your salvation in God, so that at least by that, it would be possible to correct you of your madness. If therefore you did not change at the time when I was reprimanding you, if you did not turn toward perfection at the time when I was exhorting you, if you did not fear when you were struck, how can I forgive you again?" As Silvanus continued for a long time to pray to him and promised him to correct himself, the Great one asked him for a pledge that he would not remain in the same


[p. 434] {#po.4.p434}
ἀναδεξαμένου αὐτὸν, ἐφ᾽ οἷς ὑπισχνεῖτο' κατορθοῦν, συνεχώρησεν αὐτῷ ὁ μακάριος Παχού- μος" ὁ δὲ Σιλθανὸς τυχὼν τῆς ἀφέσεως, οὕτως ἠγωνίσατο περὶ τῆς ψυχῆς αὐτοῦ, ὥστε αὐτὸν ὑπόγραμμον γενέσθαι εἰς πᾶσαν ἀρετὴν θεοσεδείας πᾶσιν τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς, " μικροῖς ἢ τε καὶ μεγάλοις.
state after he had been forgiven. When Petronios, a pure man, had stood as guarantor for what he promised, the blessed one forgave him. Silvanus, graced with forgiveness, fought with all his soul, to the point of becoming a model in every pious virtue for all the brothers, small and great.
Τὸ δὲ μέγα πλεονέκτημα αὐτοῦ τῶν ἀρετῶν ἦν ἡ ἐπ’ ἄκρον ταπεινοφροσύνη, καὶ τὸ μὴ διαλείπειν ᾽ τοὺς ὀφθαλμοὺς αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ δακρύων, ἀλλὰ συμμίγνυσθαι τῇ τροφῇ τὰ δάκρυα. Λεγόντων" δὲ αὐτῷ πολλάκις τῶν ἀδελφῶν τὸ μὴ ἐπὶ ξένων προσώπων ἢ ὅλως ἐπί τινων τοῦτο ποιεῖν, διισχυρίζετο λέγων ὅτι' « Πολλάκις ἠθέλησα ἐπικρατῆσαι τῶν" δα- κρύων, τούτων χάριν καὶ οὐκ ἴσχυσα. » Φασχόντων δὲ τῶν ἀδελφῶν ὅτι" « Δυνατόν. ἐστιν καθ᾽ ἑαυτὸν μὲν κλαίειν τὸν κατανενυγμένον, καὶ ἐν τῇ προσευχῇ ὁμοίως, μετὰ δὲ τῶν ἀδελφῶν ἐπέχειν, δύναται " γὰρ καὶ ἡ ψυχὴ χωρὶς τῶν φαινομένων τούτων δαχρύων πάν- τοτεῦ κλαίειν. Βουλόμεθα οὖν γνῶναι τί χατὰ νοῦν ἔχων ἀδιαλείπτως βρέχῃ '" τοῖς δάχρυσιν '", ὥστε πολλοὺς ἐξ ἡμῶν βλέποντάς σε αἰδεῖσθαι εἰς κόρον φαγεῖν. »
13 The height of all his perfections was his deep humility and also his continuous tears. Even when he ate with the brothers, he could not hold back his tears, but they mixed with his food. When the brothers told him not to do that at all in front of strange faces or in front of certain people, he assured them and said to them: "Often I have wanted to hold back the tears because of them and I could not." The brothers said: "He who repents could weep when he is alone, or even do it when he prays with the brothers. But at the table while eating with the brothers, the soul can weep continuously even without these visible tears. We want therefore to know what you are thinking to be constantly plunged into tears, to the point that many of us, seeing you thus, are ashamed to eat to satisfy ourselves."
" Ὁ δὲ ἔφη πρὸς τοὺς ἐρωτῶντας '" αὐτόν' « Οὐ θέλετέ'" με χλαῦσαι ἑαυτὸν, βλέποντα ἁγίους ὑπηρετοῦντάς μοι, ὧν καὶ τοῦ χονιορτοῦ τῶν ποδῶν οὐκ εἰμὶ ἄξιος. Οὐχ
14 He answered those who questioned him: "You do not want me to weep, I who see myself served by saints of whom I am not worthy of


[p. 435] {#po.4.p435}
ὀφείλω ' οὖν πενθεῖν ἐμαυτὸν, ὅτι ἀπὸ θυμελῆς ἄνθρωπος ὑπηρετοῦμαι ἀπὸ" τοιούτων ἁγίων ἀνδρῶν; Κλαίω οὖν, ἀδελφοὶ, φοδούμενος μήπως καταποθῶ ὡς Δαθὰν καὶ Ἀδηρὼν ᾽, μάλιστα ὅτι᾽ ἀπὸ ἀγνωσίας εἰς γνῶσιν γενόμενος τῆς χατὰ ψυχὴν σωτηρίας οὐχ ἐφρόντισα, ὡς χινδυνεῦσαί με ἐκδληθῆναι τῆς μονῆς, καὶ ἐγγύας δοῦναι μετὰ φρικωδεστάτων ὅρχων, ὑπὲρ τοῦ μηκέτι καταφρονῆσαι τῆς ζωῆς μου. Διὰ τοῦτο οὐκ ἐπαισχύνομαι τὰ" τοιαῦτα. μετελθεῖν᾽ οἶδα γὰρ τὰς ἁμαρτίας μου ὑπὲρ ὧν εἰ καὶ ἔκδοτον ἦν δοῦναι τὴν ψυχήν μου, οὐχ ἔστιν μοι χάρις. »
the dust of their feet! Is it not fitting to lament over myself who am a man of the theater and who find myself served by such holy men? I weep therefore, my brothers, because I fear being devoured like Dathan and Abiram and especially because at the time when I was without knowledge, I did not apply myself from the beginning to the salvation of my soul, and I found myself in danger (kindynos) of being chased from the monastery by the brothers; I had to give pledges and commit myself by formidable oaths to never again despise my life (eternal). Thus I am not ashamed to remember all that, for I know my sins and even if I gave my life in exchange,

I would not find grace (before God)." 15 While he was waging such a beautiful struggle, the Great one bore witness to him before all the brothers and said: "I testify before God, my brothers, that since the foundation of this monastery, none of all the brothers who were with me has completely imitated my character except one." When the brothers heard this, some thought that the unique man of whom he spoke was Theodore, others that it was Petronios, others Orsisios. When Theodore asked the Saint of which of them he had said that, he did not want to say; but as Theodore insisted, as well as the other great brothers who wanted to learn who that was, the Great one answered and said:


[p. 436] {#po.4.p436}
λέγων" « Ἐπ’ ἀληθείας εἰ ἤδειν ὅτι χενοδοξεῖν ' ἔχε: περὶ οὗ μέλλω λέγειν, οὐκ ἂν " συνέ- στησα τὸν τοιοῦτον, ἀλλ᾽ ἐπειδὴ οἶδα ἀχριδῶς ὅτι ἐπαινούμενος μᾶλλον ταπεινοῖ ἑαυτὸν καὶ ἐξευτελίζει" πλέον, διὰ τοῦτο πρὸς τὸ" μιμεῖσθαι ὑμᾶς τὸν κάλλιστον αὐτοῦ τρόπον, ἐπὶ πάντων ὑμῶν μακαρίσω' αὐτόν. Σὺ μὲν γὰρ, ὦ Θεόδωρε", καὶ ὅσοι εἰσὶν" κατὰ σὲ ἐν τῇ μονῇ ἀγωνιζόμενοι κατὰ μὲν τὸν χρόνον καὶ τὴν ἄσκησιν, πατέρες αὐτοῦ ἐστέ" χατὰ δὲ τὴν βαθεῖαν ταπείνωσιν χαὶ τὸ καθαρὸν τοῦ συνειδότος μέγας ἐκεῖνος ἐστίν" ὑμεῖς γὰρ δήσαντες τὸν διάδολον, ὡς στρουθίον ὑπὸ τοὺς πόδας ὑμῶν ἔθεσθε" καὶ καθ᾽ ἑκάστην χατα- πατεῖτε" αὐτὸν ὡς χῶμα, ἀλλ᾽ ἐὰν ἀμελήσητε '5 ἑαυτῶν '"' θαρροῦντες, ἀναστὰς ὁ ὑπὸ τοὺς πόδας ὑμῶν κείμενος φεύξεται μὲν, πάλιν '᾿ δὲ ἀνθυποστρέψας καθ᾽ ὑμῶν στρατεύσεται '". ὋὉ δὲ νεώτερος Σιλδανὸς, ὁ πρὸ χρόνου ὀλίγου ἐκδάλλεσθαι '" παρ᾽ ἐμοῦ '" τῆς μονῆς μέλλων διὰ τὴν ἀμέλειαν αὐτοῦ, " οὗτος ἐχειρώσατο 'ὁ παντελῶς τὸν διάδολον, καὶ ἐξηφάνισεν
"If I knew that the one of whom I want to speak would find in this praise a subject for vainglory, I would not make him known to you; but as I know that the more he is praised, the more he humbles and despises himself, I can therefore without fear praise him before you all, so that you may take him as a model. You, Theodore, and all those who struggle like you in the monastery, you have bound the demon like a sparrow, placed him under your feet, and trample him every day like dust; but if you neglect yourselves, the demon, stretched out under your feet, will rise again and fight again against you, whereas this young Silvanus who almost, a short time ago, was chased from the monastery because of his negligence, has completely subjugated the demon and killed him, so that he can no longer approach him: he has entirely conquered him by his great humility."
αὐτὸν, ὡς μὴ δύνασθαι ἔτι φανῆναι παρ᾽ αὐτῷ, τῇ ὑπερθολῇ τῆς ἑαυτοῦ '΄ ταπεινο- φροσύνης εἰς τέλος νικήσας αὐτόν. Καὶ ὑμεῖς μὲν οἱ καθ᾽ ἑξῆς; ταπεινοῦντες ἑαυτοὺς '5, ὡς προσθήκην ποιούμενοι τῆς ἑαυτῶν ἀρετῆς ἔτι '", ἑαυτοὺς ἐταπεινώσατε "6 θαρροῦντες, ἐχδέχεσθχι τὴν τῶν χαμάτων ἀμοιδὴν ", ὅτι ἀπροσωπόληπτος ὁ μισθαποδότης" οὗτος δὲ, ὅσον ἀγωνίζεται, τοσοῦτον ἑαυ-
16 You, when you humble yourselves in order to have works of justice and to increase your virtues, you work full of confidence, moreover, in what you have already done. This one, on the other hand, the more he fights, the more he considers himself of poor quality; he believes, with all his soul and all his


[p. 437] {#po.4.p437}
τὸν ' ἀδόχιμον ἀποφαίνει, ἐξ ὅλης ψυχῆς αὐτοῦ χαὶ διανοίας, ἀχρεῖον καὶ εὐτελῇ ἑαυτὸν ἐνθυμούμενος εἶναι. Διὰ τοῦτο γὰρ χαὶ τὸ δακρύειν πρόχειρον ἔχει ἐκ τοῦ πάνυ ἑαυτὸν ἐξευ- τελίζειν", καὶ τῶν ὁρωμένων" ἀνάξιον εἶναι λέγων. Ὥστε ὑμεῖς μὲν καὶ τῇ γνώμη, καὶ τῇ ὑπομονῇ, καὶ τοῖς χατὰ διαδόλου ἀμετρήτοις ᾽ ἀγῶσιν ὑμῶν " ὑπερέχετε" αὐτοῦ" ὁ δὲ τῇ ταπεινοφροσύνη ὑπεραναξέδηκεν ὑμῶν' οὐδὲν γὰρ ἄλλο οὕτως ᾿ τὸν διάδολον ἐχνευροῖ ὡς ἡ μετὰ πραχτικῆς δυνάμεως ἐξ ὅλης ψυχῆς γινομένη ταπεινοφροσύνη. » Οὕτως οὖν ἀγωνιζόμενος ὁ Σιλθανὸς ἐν ὅλοις ὄχτω ἔτεσιν, ἐτέλεσεν" τὸν καλὸν ἀγῶνα, " καταπαύσας αὐτοῦ τὸν βίον ἐν Κυρίῳ, ὡς μαρτυρῆσαι τὸν τοῦ Θεοῦ θεράποντα Παχούμιον περὶ τῆς ἐξόδου αὐτοῦ ὅτι πλῆθος ἄπειρον ἁγίων ἀγγέλων τεθέαται '5 μετὰ χαρᾶς μεγάλης καὶ ψαλμωδίας τὴν ψυχὴν αὐτοῦ λαθόντας, καὶ ὡς ἐκλεκτὴν θυσίαν, χαὶ ὡς παράδοξον ἐν ἀνθρώποις '' εὑρεθὲν θυμίαμα,
thought, that he is useless and vile. Thus he has easy tears because he debases himself much and says that he is not worthy (to enjoy) visible things. Thus you others, by knowledge and by patience, by your innumerable combats against the demon, you prevail over him, but he surpasses you by humility; for there is nothing that strikes the demon like humility coming from the whole soul and joined to the power of works." In these combats Silvanus finished his struggle in eight years and ended his life in such a way that the great servant of God bore witness, regarding his death, that innumerable troops of holy angels received his soul with great joy and psalmodies, and offered it to God as a choice host, and as extraordinary aromatics found among men.

17 On the burial of a sinner. - Once father Pachomius was going to another monastery to visit the brothers who were there. While he was going there, he met the funeral procession of a brother of this monastery who had just died; (all the brothers of the monastery) were following the procession singing, and the relatives of the dead man were also with them. The brothers saw from


[p. 438] {#po.4.p438}
πρὸς αὐτοὺς, ἀπέθεντο τὸν χράδέατον' ἐπὶ" τὴν γῆν, ἵνα ἐλθὼν εὐχὴν ποιήσῃ" ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ. Ἱστάμενοι οὖν οἱ ἀδελφοὶ ἔψαλλον μετὰ τῶν χοσμιχῶν ἄχρι τῆς παρουσίας αὐτοῦ. ᾿Ελθὼν οὖν καὶ εὐξάμενος ὁ μακάριος, ἐκέλευσεν τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς μηκέτι ψάλλειν ἔμπροσθεν αὐτοῦ" καὶ ποιήσας ἐνεχθῆναι" τὰ ἱμάτια τοῦ κοιμηθέντος ἀδελφοῦ, χελεύει ἔμπροσθεν πάντων καῆναι αὐτὰ ", καὶ χαυθέντων αὐτῶν, προσέταξεν ἀρθῆναι τὸ σκήνωμα ΄, καὶ ἄνευ ψαλμῳδίας
afar the saint coming toward them, they placed the coffin on the ground so that he might come to pray over it. When he had come and had prayed, he ordered the brothers to no longer sing over the dead man and he ordered his clothes to be brought: they brought them, and he ordered them to be burned before everyone. When they had burned them, he ordered them to carry away this corpse and to bury it without chants.

" Τῶν δὲ ἀδελφῶν σὺν τοῖς γονεῦσιν καὶ συγγενέσιν τοῦ τετελευτηχότος ῥιψάντων ἑαυτοὺς " ὑπὸ τοὺς πόδας αὐτοῦ ἐπὶ τῷ συγχωρηθῆναι ψάλλεσθαι αὐτῷ ", οὐκ ἠνέσχετο τοῦτο ποιῆσαι, τῶν δὲ γονέων αὐτοῦ λεγόντων' « Τί ποιεῖς, ὦ πάτερ, τὸ καινὸν τοῦτο χαὶ ἀθέμιτον πρᾶγμα, καὶ ξένον τῆς χριστιανυκὴς παραδόσεως, προσάπτων τῷ τέκνῳ ἡμῶν ""; Οὐ πρέπει τῇ σῇ ἁγιότητι τοιαύτην ἀσπλαγχνίαν ἐπιδείχνυσθαι "' ἐπὶ νεκρῷ, ὡς χαὶ βαρθάρων ὡμότητα ὑπερδαίνειν τὸ γινόμενον. Ποῖος γὰρ ἐχθρὸς ὁρῶν τοῦ ἀντιδίκου αὐτοῦ τὸ σκήνωμα, ἀκίνητον καὶ ἄφωνον κείμενον, εἰς συμπάθειαν 'ἡ ἐλθὼν οὐκ φχτείρησεν ἢ ἄν; νῦν δὲ "" καινότερον θέαμα "ἢ εἴδομεν παρ’ ὑμῖν τοῖς Χριστιανοῖς, ἢ οὐδὲ παρὰ βαρόάροις ὥφθη ποτέ. Ὄνειδος '" γὰρ ἀνεξάλειπτον " προσάγεις τῷ γένει ἡμῶν διὰ τῆς τοιαύτης σου '"ἷ ἀσπλαχνίας. Εἴθε" μὴ εἴδαμέν'"" σε σήμερον, ἵνα μὴ αἰώνιον μῶμον διὰ σοῦ ὃ οἶκος ἡμῶν ἐχληρονόμησεν ᾽ὁ, Εἴθε" μὴ μετῆλθεν τὸν ἀγριώτατον χαὶ ἀσυμπαθὴ βίον τοῦτον ὁ ἄθλιος.
As the brothers, the parents, and the relatives of the dead man threw themselves at his feet and begged him to let them sing over him, he did not permit it. His parents said to him: "What are you doing, O father? You impose on our son this new (and) illegal thing. It does not befit your holiness to show such inhumanity against this dead man. For (death) can change even the hardness of barbarians into sorrow. The enemy himself who sees the corpse of his adversary lying without movement and without voice knows well often how to have pity on it. We see now among you others, Christians, a new spectacle that has never been seen among the barbarians. By this hardness, you imprint on our race an indelible stain. Would to God that we had not seen you today, so that our house would not inherit this shameful stain forever! Would to God that our wretched son had not sought this cruel life, for he would not have


[p. 439] {#po.4.p439}
οὗτος υἱὸς ἡμῶν" οὐ γὰρ ἂν κατέλειπεν ' ἡμῖν τὴν αἰώνιον ταύτην ὀδύνην. Εἰ γοῦν '"" καὶ τὰ ἱμάτια αὐτοῦ καυθῆναι ἐποίησας, ἡ τὸν ψαλμὸν συγχώρησον λεχθῆναι. » ᾿Αποχριθεὶς δὲ ὁ μαχάριος Παχούμιος εἶπεν αὐτοῖς" « ᾿Αληθῶς, ἀδελφοὶ, πλεῖον “ ὑμῶν οἰκτείρω ᾿ τὸν κείμενον τοῦτον χαὶ περισσοτέραν ὑμῶν ἢ φροντίδα ποιούμενος αὐτοῦ ὡς πατὴρ προσέταξα τοῦτο γενέσθαι. Ὑμεῖς γὰρ τοῦ φαινομένου σκήνους δοχεῖτε φροντίδα ποιεῖσθαι", ὅπερ καὶ μὴ βουλομένων ὑμῶν εἰς γὴν λυθὲν, χάριτας ὑμῖν οὐχ ὁμολογήσειδ. ᾿Εγὼ δὲ ὑπὲρ τῆς ψυχῆς αὐτοῦ ἀγωνίζομαι" ἐὰν γὰρ ψάλλητε '" αὐτῷ, πλείονες '' " αὐτὸν "ἢ διαδέξονται χολάσεις, ἀπαιτουμένων "ἢ ὑπὲρ τοῦ ψαλλομένου λόγων "", ὅτι οὐκ ἀπῆλθεν ἔχων" τῶν ψαλμῶν τὴν δύναμιν. Ἐἰ μὲν οὖν ἀληθῶς θέλετε 'ὁ προσθήκην αὐτῷ ποιῆσαι τῶν αἰωνίων ὀδυνῶν, καὶ δὴ "7 ψάλατε αὐτῷ" "" πλέον ὀδυνώμενος '" χαταράσεται ᾽ ὑμῖν, Εἰδὼς οὖν ἐγὼ τὸ συμφέρον τῆς ψυχῆς αὐτοῦ, οὐκ ἐπιμελοῦμαι τοῦ νεχροῦ σώματος αὐτοῦ, τοῦ
left us this eternal suffering. 'We ask you therefore', although you have had his clothes burned, to let us say the chant." 18 He answered and said to them: "In truth, my brothers, I have more pity than you for him who lies here; it is because I take great care of him, like a father, that I have commanded this. You, you take care of this visible body, but I, I struggle for his soul. For if you sing over him, numerous torments will come to him; the account of them will be demanded of the one for whom one sings, because he did not depart having the right to the chant of the psalms. If therefore you want to increase his eternal pains, sing; for I tell you the truth: if you sing over him, as he will suffer more from it, he will curse you because of (these) chants. Because I know what is useful to his soul; I do not...
μετ᾽ ὀλίγον εἰς γῆν ἀναλυομένου "". Ἐὰν γὰρ συγχωρήσω ὑμῖν ψάλλειν "", ὡς ἀνθρωπάρεσχος εὑρίσχομαι παρὰ τῷ Θεῷ ὅτι, ἕνεχεν ἀνθρώπων πληροφορίας, τὸ συμφέρον τῆς ψυχῆς παρι- δὼν, χατεφρόνησα τῆς μελλούσης ἐν χρίσει κολάσεως "ὃ, Πηγὴ γὰρ ὑπάρχων ἀγαθότητος "ἡ ὁ Θεὸς, ζητεῖ προφάσεις δι’ ὧν δράξασθα: δύναται τοῦ πηγάζειν εἰς ἡμᾶς τὰ τῆς "" ἑαυτοῦ
If I let you sing, God will accuse me of having sacrificed to human respect for having neglected, in view of human tranquility, the things useful to the soul which will be tortured at the judgment. Because God is a source of goodness, He only asks for pretexts in order to seize them and to make the floods of His grace flow toward us. If therefore we, who have been graced by God with the knowledge of divine medicine, do not bring to every suffering


[p. 440] {#po.4.p440}
χρηστότητος ῥεῖθρα '. " ᾿Εὰν οὖν ἡμεῖς οἱ παρὰ Θεοῦ χαταξιωθέντες τῆς θείας αὐτοῦ ἰατρικῆς ἐπιστήμης " ἔμπειροι εἶναι, μὴ τὸ ἐπιτήδειον " βοήθημα ἑκάστῳ πάθει προσφέρω- μεν᾽, ὡς καταφρονηταὶ εἰκότως ἀχουσόμεθα. τὸ θεῖον λόγιον ἢ" Ἴδετε οἱ καταφρονηταὶ καὶ θαυμάσατε" χαὶ ἀφανίσθητε". Διὰ τοῦτο παραχαλῶ ὑμᾶς πρὸς τὸ χουφισθῆναι αὐτὸν τῆς μελλούσης τιμωρίας, χωρὶς ψαλμῶν ἄρατε αὐτόν" δύναται γὰρ ὁ Θεὸς ἀγαθὸς ὧν, ὑπὲρ ταύτης τῆς γεναμένης ᾽ αὐτῷ ἀτιμίας ", ἄνεσιν αὐτῷ παρασχεῖν, καὶ εἰς ζωὴν ἀνακαλέσασθαι αὐτόν. Εἰ γὰρ ἤκουέν μου, πολλάκις νουθετηθεὶς παρ᾽ ἐμοῦ, οὐκ ἂν ἔφθασεν εἰς ταῦτα. » Καὶ ταῦτα
the remedy that befits it, it is with justice that we will hear, like (the) contemners, what is written: Contemners, see! be astonished and disappear. I advise you therefore, to spare him punishment, to bury him without chants. For God who is good can, for this lack of attention regarding the (dead man), give him rest and call him to life. If he had listened to me when I often reprimanded him, he would not have come to this." When the blessed one had spoken thus, they carried the dead man to the mountain without singing and he was buried.
εἰπόντος τοῦ ἁγίου, ἀπηνέχθη" εἰς τὸ ὄρος χωρὶς ψαλμοῦ χαὶ ἐτάφη "ἡ. 19. The Saint spent a few days in this monastery instructing, and teaching the fear of God to each of the brothers and how one must fight the demon, his traps and his frauds and render vain in advance, by an easy foresight and with the support of the Lord, everything that he schemes against us.

20 Burial of a holy brother who died. - While he was still there, they told him that a certain brother who was from the monastery called in Greek "the pasture of the geese", had fallen ill and wanted to see him to be blessed by him before dying. At these words, the man of God rose

αὐτοῖς, Γενομένου δὲ αὐτοῦ ὡς' ἀπὸ δύο μιλίων τῆς μονῆς ἐκείνης, ἤκουσεν φωνὴν ἱερὰν ἐν τῷ ἀέρι, χαὶ ἀνανεύσας εἶδεν" τὴν ψυχὴν τοῦ χκαχουμένου ἀδελφοῦ μετὰ τῶν ἁγίων ᾿ ἀγγέλων ψάλλουσαν χαὶ εἰς τὴν μαχαρίαν ζωὴν τοῦ ' Θεοῦ ' ἀπαγομένην.
and left. Two miles from this monastery, the Saint heard a sacred voice in the air. He raised his eyes and saw the soul of this brother who had been ill: it was singing with the holy angels and was being led to the blessed and divine life.

21 The brothers who accompanied him heard and saw nothing, as he stopped and looked for a long time toward the east, they said to him: "Why do you stop, O father? let us hurry to find him still alive." He said to them: "We will no longer find him there, for I see him carried toward eternal life; go therefore, my sons, to your monastery." The brothers asked him how he saw the soul of this dead brother; he told them the very mode (of his vision); after having heard it, they went to their monastery, and learned with accuracy from the brothers of the monastery (that he had died) at the hour that the Great one had told them. They knew thus that the things said by the Saint regarding this dead brother were true. 22 Of what he heard the demons say in the air when he was traveling through the desert to his monastery. - As this holy 'elder' was traveling to his monastery and was near the desert which is called Amnon, legions of demons rose against him and followed him to the right and to the left, while others ran before him and said: "Here is the man blessed by God." They did this to strive to sow in him vainglory.


[p. 442] {#po.4.p442}
γνοὺς αὐτῶν τὴν χαχουργίαν, ὅσον ἐχεῖνοι ἔχραζον εὐφημοῦντες αὐτὸν, τοσοῦτον αὐτὸς ἀναβοῶν πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν ἐξωμολογεῖτο ' τὰς ἑαυτοῦ ἁμαρτίας, ἀνατρέπων τὴν κακουργίαν τῶν δαιμόνων ", ἔλεγεν δὲ καὶ πρὸς αὐτούς: « Οὐ δύνασθέ" με συναρπάσαι' πρὸς χενο- δοξίαν, ὦ ἀνοσιώτατοι" οἶδα γάρ μου τὰς ἀνομίας, δι’ ἂς ὀφείλω" πάντοτε χλαίειν περὶ τῆς αἰωνίου κολάσεως. Οὐ δέομαι" οὖν τῆς παρ᾽ ὑμῶν ψευδολογίας χαὶ δολερᾶς ἀπάτης" ὑμῶν γὰρ τὸ ἔργον ἀπωλεία ψυχῶν “ ἐστιν. Οὐ συναρπάζομα! οὖν ὑμῶν τοῖς ἐπαίνοις, οἶδα γὰρ τὸ σοφὸν τῆς κακίας τῆς ἀνοσίας" ὑμῶν γνώμης. »
glory, but he knew their wickedness, and the more they shouted, the more he implored God and confessed his sins. When he had thwarted the wickedness of the evil demons, he said to them: "You cannot drag me into vainglory, O wicked ones, for I know my iniquities for which I must weep always because of eternal punishment. I have no need, therefore, of your deceitful words and your tricks, for your work is the perdition of souls; I have no concern, therefore, for your praises, for I know the wickedness of your iniquitous spirit."
Ταῦτα δὲ τοῦ ἁγίου Παχουμίου λέγοντος πρὸς τοὺς δαίμονας, οὐδὲ οὕτως ἐπαύσαντο τῆς ἀναιδείας αὐτῶν, ἀλλὰ παρέμειναν αὐτῷ ἕως ἤγγισεν εἰσελθεῖν εἰς τὴν μονὴν αὐτοῦ" τότε ὡς ἀσπαζόμενοι αὐτὸν ἀνεχώρησαν".
23 When this saint * Pachomius had said these words to the demons, they did not even put an end to their impudence, but clung to the blessed one until the moment he approached his monastery."

24 What he did after arriving at his monastery. - As the brothers were coming out to meet him and greeting him, a child of the monastery came out with the brothers 'to greet the saint', and came to approach him, saying: "In truth, O father, since you went out to visit the brothers until now, neither vegetables nor peas have been cooked for us." The holy old man answered him cheerfully and said: "Do not complain, my son, I will see to it that from now on they are cooked for you." When he had walked through the monastery and arrived at the kitchen, he found the cook busy weaving mats and said to him: "For


[p. 443] {#po.4.p443}
ἀδελφοῖς λάχανα '; » Ὁ δὲ ἔφη" « Δύο μῆνας. » Καὶ λέγει αὐτῷ" « Τί τοῦτο πεποίηχας ", τῶν ἐντολῶν καὶ χανόνων τῶν ἁγίων πατέρων χελευόντων χατὰ σάδόατον καὶ χυριακὴν ἑψεῖσθαι" λάχανα' τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς; » Ὁ δὲ φησὶν πρὸς αὐτόν" « ᾿Αληθῶς,᾿ πάτερ, ἤθελον χαθ᾽ ἑκάστην ἡμέραν αὐτοῖς ἑψεῖσθα:", ἀλλ᾽ ἐπειδὴ εἶδον ὅτι ἑψούμενον τὸ λάχανον οὐχ ἐσθίεται, τῶν ἀδελφῶν σχεδὸν πάντων ἐγκρατευομένων καὶ μὴ ἐσθίοντων᾽ τὸ ἔψημα, ἵνα οὖν μὴ τὸ μετὰ τοσούτου χόπου γινόμενον ἀνάλωμα, μηδενὸς ἐσθίοντος΄, βληθῇ ἔξω, τεσσαράκοντα γὰρ ξέστας ἐλαίου βάλλομεν τῆς ἡμέρας εἰς τὸ σύνηθες ἔψημα " τῶν ἀδελφῶν, ἐπεὶ οὖν εἶδον" ὅτι οὐκ ἐσθίουσιν, τούτου " χάριν οὐχ ἕἔψησα 'δ, ἄτοπον ἡγησάμενος ' τὸ βάλλειν τοσαῦτα ἀναλώματα. ἔξω. Διά τε τοῦτο, πρὸς τὸ '" μὴ ἀργόν με καθῆσθαι '᾽, ἐργά-- ζομαι ψιαθία μετὰ τῶν ἀδελφῶν, λογισάμενος ἕνα καὶ μόνον ἀρκεῖν τῷ μαγειρείῳ "᾿ πρὸς τὸ" χαταρτῦσαι 'δ μικρὰ βρώματα τοῖς μεταλαμθάνουσιν ἀδελφοῖς, ἅπερ ἐστίν λαμψάναι "΄ μετὰ ὄξους, καὶ ἐλαῖαι '", χαὶ σκορδούμας ᾽", καὶ λεπτολάχανα. »
how long have you not cooked vegetables for the brothers?" He replied: "For two months." The Great one said to him: "You have done this, when the laws and canons of the holy Fathers order a dish of vegetables to be made for the brothers on Saturday and Sunday!" He replied: "In truth, O father, I wanted to cook them every day, but I saw that the cooked vegetables were not eaten because all the brothers, so to speak, were restraining themselves and not eating what was cooked; 'only the children ate this dish'. So that the expense incurred with so much trouble * would not be thrown away, since no one was eating it - for we put forty setiers of oil into the brothers' food every day - when I saw that it was not being eaten, I did not cook any more; I did not think it permissible for me to throw away so much expense, so, not to remain idle, I work on mats with the brothers, for I thought that one person is enough in the kitchen to prepare a little food for the brothers, such as herbs macerated in vinegar, olives, green plants from the mountain, and herbs from the garden."

"Five hundred." He replied: "Bring them here, so that I may count them." When they had been brought, he had them thrown into the fire, and when they were burned, he said to them: "Just as you abandoned the rule that was given to you for the conduct of the brothers, in order to (follow) a satanic idea, so I too destroy without pity the work of your hands by burning it in the fire to teach you what it is to despise the laws of the fathers that were given for the benefit of souls. How much help you have taken away from the brothers by not cooking * the dishes! Do you not know that a man may desire (these dishes)? And he who abstains from them for God will receive no small reward; but he who does not have the power (to desire), but (abstains) by force and necessity because he has nothing, abstains in vain and asks in vain.

I would truly like to cook many foods every day and place them before the brothers, so that by being temperate every day and depriving themselves of the things given to them, they might take a daily increase in perfection. If it happens that a sick man does not ask to go to the infirmary, but comes to the common table to eat the vegetables that are usually given and finds none, what will happen? Will he not (ἄρα) be scandalized not to find at the common table what he needs? Do you not know that children especially * cannot remain in perfection if they do not find a little pleasure or a little satisfaction?"

a great stench came from them to the old man; and he did not know the cause of this stench that emanated from them, because he was constantly speaking to them face to face and could not therefore pray to God to reveal the cause to him. He saw that their speech was nourished and their mind accustomed to the (holy) Books, and he was therefore not convinced of their spiritual stench. After he had spoken to them at length about the divine Scriptures, the ninth hour arrived and they rose to go to their homes. They were invited by the Great one to eat there and did not accept: they were concerned * to arrive home before sunset. They therefore prayed, 'greeted' us, and left.

He saw again an innumerable crowd of brothers who were in a deep and dried-up valley; many of them sought to get out of this valley and could not. Many, because of the great thickness of the darkness, met face to face and did not realize it; many fell from weakness; others shouted with a lamentable voice. A few barely with great labor were able to get out of this valley and, when they * had climbed it, the light immediately came to them. As soon as they had arrived at the light, they rendered long thanksgivings to God.

I have worked in vain (εἰκῆ) and uselessly. I have fought in vain (εἰκῆ). Remember, Lord, my labors and those of all these brothers who with all their soul * let themselves be led; remember that you promised me to preserve until the end of this world this spiritual seed. You know it, Lord, since I put on the habit of the monks, I have never satiated myself with anything on this earth, not even water."

ῥίψας ἑαυτὸν ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς, ἡτήσατο ἔλεος παρὰ τοῦ Θεοῦ λέγων' « Κύριε παντοκράτωρ, τὸ ἔλεός σου χατάπεμψον ἐπ᾽ ἐμὲ, καὶ μηδέποτε ἀφέλῃς αὐτὸ ἀπ᾽ ἐμοῦ, ὅτι οἶδα " κἀγὼ ὅτι ἄνευ τοῦ ἐλέους σου τὰ σύμπαντα οὐ δύναται" εἶναι. » Ταῦτα αὐτοῦ προσευχομένου, ἰδοὺ δύο ἄγγελοι τοῦ Θεοῦ ἐπέστησαν αὐτῷ χαὶ ἦν μετ᾽ ὁ αὐτῶν νεώτερος, ἀνεκλάλητον καὶ ἄφατον" ἔχων τὴν θέαν τοῦ προσώπου, χαὶ ἐπὶ τῆς κεφαλῆς αὐτοῦ στέφανον ἐξ ἀχανθῶν, Καὶ ἀναστήσαντες οἱ ἄγγελοι τὸν Παχούμιον εἶπον αὐτῷ" « ᾿Επειδὴ ἠτήσω τὸν " Θεὸν, κατόπεμψέν" σοι τὸ ἔλεος αὐτοῦ᾽ ἰδοὺ αὐτός ἐστιν τὸ ἔλεος αὐτοῦ, ὁ Κύριος τῆς δόξης ὁ Χριστὸς, ὁ μονογενὴς υἱὸς αὐτοῦ, ὃν ἀπέστειλεν τῷ χόσμῳ σωτῆρα" καὶ ἐσταυρώσατε αὐτὸν, καὶ στέφανον ἐξ ἀκανθῶν περιεθήκατε αὐτῷ "Ὑ, » Εἶπεν δὲ ὁ ΤΙαχοῦμιος πρὸς αὐτόν
my mercies." At these words, the blessed one immediately prostrated himself on the ground and implored divine mercy, saying: "Lord almighty, send me your mercies and never take them away from me, for I know that without your mercies nothing can exist." When he had said this, two angels of God immediately stood near him and a young man was with them who had an ineffable face and an inexpressible look; on his head was a crown of thorns. The angels raised Pachomius and said to him: "Because you asked God to send you his mercy, behold, that one is his mercy, the Lord of glory, Jesus Christ, his only son, whom he sent to the world and whom you crucified, and you put on his head a crown of thorns."
« Δέομαί σου, τῆς ἀχράντου φύσεως δέσποτα, οὐκ ἐσταύρωσά σε ἐγώ. » Ὁ δὲ νεώτερος ὑπομειδιάσας " εἶπεν'" αὐτῷ" « Οἶδα ὅτι σύ με οὐκ ἐσταύρωσας, ἀλλ᾽ οἱ πατέρες ὑμῶν. Θάρσει δὲ, ὅτι ἡ ῥίζα τοῦ σπέρματός σου τοῦ πνευματικοῦ ἕως τοῦ αἰῶνος τούτου οὐ μὴ ἐχλείπῃ "". Pachomius said to the young man: "I beg you, * Lord, as well as your holy nature, I did not * crucify you!" The young man's face brightened a little, and he said to him: "I know that you did not crucify me, but your fathers crucified me; have confidence therefore: the root of your seed will never disappear, and your race will be preserved on earth until the consummation of the world. The small number of those who will be saved at that time from the abundant darkness will have a conduct higher in perfection than those of now. For now you are to them like a light before their eyes, and they conduct themselves excellently, supported as they are by your light. Those who will follow them, who will be on a dark earth, if by a good


[p. 451] {#po.4.p451}
εἰ" ἐχουσίως ἐν ἀγαθῷ φρονήματι, μηδενὸς αὐτοῖς" ὁδηγοῦντος ἐπὶ τὴν ἀλήθειαν, ἀποπηδή- σαντες τῆς σχοτίας" τοῦ ψεύδους τὴν διχαιοσύνην μετέλθωσιν ἀμὴν λέγω ὅ ὅτι μετὰ τῶν ἄκρως νῦν πολιτευομένων χαὶ ἀμέμπτως εὑρεθήσονται τῆς αὐτῆς ἀπολαύοντες σωτηρίας. » Καὶ ταῦτα εἰπὼν εἰς οὐρανὸν ἀνῆλθεν τοῦ ἀέρος φωτισθέντος.
will and an effective thought, and without anyone leading them, they run to the truth and approach from the darkness toward certainty, in truth I tell you, they will be delivered with those who now have an eminent and blameless conduct, and will be judged worthy of the same salvation." When he had said this, he went to heaven; the heaven opened and the air became luminous 'to the point that one cannot recount the splendor of this light with the help of human words!'

31 While the Great one was in admiration of what had been said to him, immediately they knocked (to call) the brothers to the night meeting. Instructive words that he addressed to the gathered brothers. - When the brothers had gathered * at the night assembly and the night office was finished, the brothers sat down to listen to his word. He opened his mouth and said to them: * "My brothers, as long as breath remains in your body, fight for your salvation, before that hour comes when we must weep for ourselves. Let us practice virtue with ardor: for, I tell you, if you knew the goods of heaven and the glory that is reserved for the saints, the torments of those who have abandoned God, the punishments reserved for the negligent and especially for those who know the truth and do not conduct themselves toward it as is fitting to inherit the beatitude reserved for the saints; you would flee the sufferings of the

"Go therefore to the tombs and see that the substance of man is nothing. Why does man who is dust give himself to vainglory? Why does he boast, he who is only rot? Let us therefore weep for ourselves when there is time, for fear that at the moment of our departure (from this world) we find ourselves reduced to asking God for another time to repent. Oh! how unhappy and full of repentance will be that soul which has abandoned the world and given itself to God without living in a manner consistent with its promise! Let us not, therefore, my brothers, let this world, which is small and contemptible and like the passing shadow, take away from us the blessed and immortal life. In truth, I fear * that your fathers according to the flesh, who live in the world and who struggle in the cares and sufferings of the world and who believe that you are close to the Lord and that you have taken from here a pledge of entry into eternal life, * will find no more assistance than you for the world to come and then will condemn us and say what is written: How have you worked to be so humiliated? the fire has been lit upon you, your branches have corrupted,

Lord. 32 * Now that you have heard this, my brothers, let us work with all our soul by placing death before our eyes at every hour, 'and by considering the formidable punishments that must lead the mind to feeling, after which the soul carries while weeping the weight (of the body), for (the mind) opens the eyes of the soul and leads it without delay from earthly things to God. Moreover, when humility has brought about the contempt of (earthly) things, (the mind) leads the soul to be free from vainglory, modest, and delivered * from every earthly thought.' Our soul therefore, my brothers, will do philosophy every day to our heavy body, and when we go to lie down in the evening it will say to each of the members of the body: "O feet, as long as you can stand straight and set yourselves in motion, before dying and being motionless, obey with good will your master." It will say to the hands: "O hands, an hour will come when you will remain motionless, when you will be bound together and when you will no longer have a movement; why therefore, before falling at that hour, do you cease being stretched toward the Lord? To

the whole body, the soul will say: "O body, before we separate and move away from each other, 'before I descend * into Sheol and receive eternal bonds under the darkness, before you change into your first being, that you resolve into the earth to become there rot and corruption, stand upright with courage to worship the Lord without ceasing, show by (your) tears my (good) feelings, make known to your master your free servitude; carry me so that I may confess God with good will, before you are carried yourself by others; you must not sleep and rest so that you condemn me to eternal punishments. If you anoint yourselves thus (for the struggle) every day, you will in truth be true temples of God: and, when God dwells in you, the ruses of Satan will no longer be able to harm you; instead of thousands of teachers, the Word of God will dwell in you; it will instruct you much better and will teach you above all his knowledge. The holy and divine Spirit, pure and without stain, will teach you everything that human speech cannot say; as the apostle said: We do not know what to ask nor how to ask, but the spirit prays for us with ineffable groanings, etc. It would be possible to tell you many other things very

33 How, even in a time of famine, he did not want to accept a loan of wheat for the use of his monastery. - Once when there was a famine in the time of Pachomius and the brothers did not have wheat, or even when one could not find wheat, so to speak, throughout all of Egypt, the holy old man sent * a brother to travel through the cities and towns and ask for wheat to buy, he gave him a hundred denarii for the purchase of wheat. When the one who was sent had traveled through many places, he arrived at a city named Hermotin. By an effect of Providence, he found there a man, a magistrate in this city, who was pure and fearing God and who had heard of the conduct of Saint Pachomius and the brothers. This magistrate was in charge of the wheat of the public treasury. The brother went to find him and asked him to sell him grain for a hundred denarii. He replied: "In truth, my brother, if I had my wheat, I would take it from my children and I would give it to you, for I have heard of your divine and excellent conduct." Listen therefore to what I am going to tell you:
μου ὃ μέλλω σοι λέγειν, ἀδελφέ ᾿ὃ. Δημόσιον σῖτον ἔχω κείμενον, χαὶ τέως ἄρτι οὐ ζητεῖται "'
I have at my place the wheat of the public treasury, and the governor will not claim it from me now; if you want to take it until the time of the harvest,

I can make those of the public treasury wait; if you know that you will be able to return the wheat at that time, take what you want." Then the brother said: "I do not want you to act thus toward me, for I cannot bring back the whole quantity that I am going to take; but if you wish, * give me some, at whatever price you want, for these hundred denarii; for if you can make those of the public treasury wait until the harvest, you do well." The latter said to him: "I can certainly make them wait, and not only for these hundred (denarii), but if you want to take more wheat for as much again, you will please me in this. Only, pray for me." As the brother replied: "We have no other money than that," the magistrate, hearing him, said to him: "Do not worry about it; when you are able, you will bring me the money."

wheat and how these grains had been bought, he sent to the boat and said: "Do not carry any grain of this wheat into the monastery, and he who bought it shall not come near me before having taken it back to where he took it. He has greatly sinned in doing this and especially in taking wheat for another hundred denarii, which I had not commanded him to do; but, obeying his own will, he has loved * excess, and, inflamed by the passion of avarice, he has made us slaves and debtors. He did not use the donor's kindness with moderation, but, as if driven by avarice, he brought more wheat than we need; and, of his own authority (αὐθεντία), he borrowed what we cannot return. And not only that, but if he had happened to die on the way back, or if the boat had sunk, what would we have done? Would we not all have had to be reduced to servitude?"

The Great one said to him: "What is the thing he did wrong?" The shoemaker replied and said: "I gave him the sandals and other objects to sell, and I told him what was their

price. But he sold them for more and brought me a price three times higher than the price designated by me!" When the Great one heard this, he called the brother and said to him: "Why did you do that?" He replied to him: "O our father, I indicated to the men who bought from me the prices that this brother had told me to take, and they said to me: Brother, (if these objects) were not stolen, they would be worth a higher price. I said to them, full of shame: They are not stolen, but I was commanded to sell them at this price; give for them what you want. They therefore gave me what they pleased, without me * counting the obols they gave me." The Great one, hearing him, said to him: "You have greatly sinned, because you loved the surplus. Run quickly and return the surplus of the price to those who gave it to you, then come to do penance for this sin and remain in the monastery applied to manual labor; for it is not fitting for you, my son, to take up the same occupation again." This brother did as the Great one had told him; then the old man appointed to the service of all the affairs of the monastery Saint Zaki, a good man "and who surpasses all human praise by the practice of good actions.

36 On a solitary who dwelt in the monastery and who was seized by an untimely desire for martyrdom - There was a certain brother who led the eremitic life alone, one of those who were very famous. When he learned of the divine conduct of our father Saint Pachomius, he asked him to receive him into the monastery. When the Great one had received him and he had spent some time among the brothers, he desired martyrdom at a time when the world was at peace, the Church prosperous and, by the grace of God, tranquil; when the blessed and Christ-bearing Constantine was reigning. He implored the blessed one constantly and said: "Pray for me, our father, so that I may become a martyr." * The Great one warned him not to let this thought rise in his heart anymore, for he said to him: "Brother, endure with valor and without stain the labor of the monks, by directing your life as it pleases Christ, and you will have a part in heaven with the martyrs." As his desire strengthened every day in this sense and he importuned the saint on this subject, the latter, to extinguish

37 At the end of two years, some brothers were sent by the Great one to the village situated above them, in order to gather reeds there for the mats of the monastery. This village was near the barbarians called Blemmyes. As the brothers were still on an island where there were many reeds, * the blessed one sent them this brother who wanted to suffer martyrdom, to carry some provisions to the brothers; he recommended that he take care and said to him with mystery what is written: Behold now the (particularly) favorable time, behold now the day of salvation. Give no cause of scandal to anyone, so that our ministry may not be the object of any

δαπανῶν ἐπορεύετο πρὸς τοὺς ἀδελφούς. Ὁ δὲ ἰδὼν γυμνὰ τὰ ξίφη '", καὶ τὸ ἦθος αὐτῶν τὸ ᾽ὁ ἄγριον δειλιάσας "", λαθὼν τὸν οἶνον ἔσπεισεν "" τοῖς εἰδώλοις '" αὐτῶν καὶ ἔφαγεν μετ᾽ αὐτῶν ἐκ τῶν χρεῶν τῶν εἰδωλοθύτων 'δ, Κι αὶ φοδηθεὶς τὸν τοῦ σώματος ἀπο- θανεῖν "ὁ θάνατον, τὴν ἀθάνατον ἀπέκτεινεν" ψυχὴν, τὸν δεσπότην πάντων ἀρνησάμενος
When the barbarians saw him coming with the donkey, they began to mock him and to say: "Monk, come worship our gods." They killed animals and made libations to their gods, then they called the monk and pressed him to make libations with them. As he did not want to do it, they rose in fury, approached him holding their naked swords, and threatened him, if he did not want to sacrifice to their gods and make libations to them, to kill him immediately. When he saw the naked swords and their savage spirit, he immediately took wine and made a libation to their gods, then ate with them from the sacrifices made to the idols. Because he feared the death * of the body, he killed the immortal soul when he denied God almighty. When he had done that, "the Blemmyes let him go.

crime, that is to say the iniquity he had committed, and he tore his clothes. After having struck his face many times, he came to the monastery. The blessed one knew what had happened, and he went out to meet him in great anguish. When (the brother) saw him approaching, he threw his face to the ground, cried with tears, and said: "I have sinned against God and against you, O father, by not listening to your good counsel nor your reprimand. If I had listened to you, I would not have suffered this." The Great one, hearing him, said to him: "Rise, wretch; you have deprived yourself of such goods, 'O shameful one!' in truth a crown was offered to you and you rejected it far from you. You were ready to be counted with the holy martyrs, and you have cut yourself off from their blessed society. Our Lord Jesus Christ was near with his holy angels and wanted to place the crown" on your head, and you denied him for (to keep) a respite of an instant. You were afraid of this death that you will always have to undergo without wanting to; you abandoned your God and you lost eternal life. Where are your previous words? Where is your desire (for martyrdom)?" * During this time he was saying: "I have sinned

As he was saying this and weeping, the Great one said to him: "You, O wretch, have made yourself completely a stranger to the Lord, but the Lord is good and has never raised his anger as a (lasting) monument, for he wants mercy and he can submerge our sins in the depths of the sea. "As far as the heaven is from the earth, so far does he remove from us our iniquities and our sins," for he does not want the death of the sinner but his conversion; (he asks) the one who has fallen not to remain in his fall but to rise, and the one who has turned away not to go far but to return immediately to him. Therefore do not suppress your hope, for you have hope of salvation. It is written: If you cut a tree, it will bloom again. If therefore you want to listen to me in everything that I will tell you, God will forgive you." The brother said to him while weeping: "I listen to you from now on in everything, O our father." The Great one ordered him to withdraw, to shut himself up alone in a cell and to converse with no one until his death, to eat

each day * bread and salt only, * and to drink water all the time of his life, to make two mats each day, to watch as much as he could, to pray as much as possible, and not to cease weeping. The latter went away as the blessed one had told and ordered him; he did double everything he had said and he saw no one, if not only the Great one and Theodore, and some of the other great old men. He lived ten years in these exercises and died in divine grace, after the Great one had given good testimony of his salvation.

39 Of an apparition that they saw at night while walking in the monastery. - Once the Great one, with Theodore whom he loved, was walking through the monastery during the night. They suddenly saw a great apparition (φαντασία) fertile in deception. What appeared to them had the aspect (σχῆμα) of a woman; her beauty was ineffable, to the point that no one can describe her beauty, nor her costume (σχῆμα), nor the aspect of the apparition. Therefore Theodore who saw this apparition was moved by it and his face was altered. When the blessed one saw that he was afraid, he said to him: "Have confidence in the Lord, Theodore, and do not

Pachomius questioned her and said to her: "Where are you from? who are you? and whom do you want to test?" She replied and said: "I am the daughter of the demon, I possess all his power and all the phalanx (φάλαγξ) of the demons is subject to me. I am she who makes the holy stars fall to the earth, I am she who stripped Judas of the apostolic power. I have therefore received power to fight against you, O Pachomius; for I could not bear the mockery of the demons: no one as much as you has repelled me, you have made me trampled underfoot by the young, by the old, and by the adolescents; you have gathered such a crowd against me, by surrounding them with the unshakeable wall of the fear of God, * that my servants no longer have the courage to approach any of

40 Pachomius asking her: "Did you come to tempt me alone, as you said, or (to tempt) others as well?" she said to him: "You and all those who resemble you." Pachomius said to him: "Therefore also Theodore." She replied: "I have received power against you and against Theodore, but I cannot in any way approach you." As he was asking her: "Why?" she said to him: "If I fight against you, I am more useful to you than harmful, and especially to you, Pachomius, who have been judged worthy to see the glory of God with the eyes of the body; but you will not always live near those to whom you now serve as a wall and render service thanks to your prayers. There will come a time after your death - you who now guard them against me - where I will dominate them. It is you who make me trampled underfoot * by this multitude of monks."

The Great one said to her: "You have lied on your impure head, for you do not know the future in advance; God alone has foreknowledge, but you are the queen of lies." She replied to him: "Yes, I know nothing by foreknowledge, as you said; for it belongs only to God to know in advance. It is by a certain conjecture that I told you that I knew it." The blessed one said to her: "How do you conjecture it?" She said to him: "With the help of past events, we conjecture those that have not yet taken place." He said to her: "How?" She said to him: "I know that everything, * which begins with love and science, is fortified by the object to which one applies oneself, especially for the divine plantation and the heavenly vocation which is founded by the will of God on prodigies and signs and which fortifies by various prodigies those who come to it; but when this beginning grows old and takes on age, it ceases to grow, then the growth (itself) falls consumed by time, exhausted by illness or blunted by negligence."

σοι" ἀφ᾽ οὗ ἡ παντοχρατορικὴ δύναμις τοῦ Σωτῆρος ἐπὶ γῆς ἐφάνη, ἐξενευρώθημεν ἡμεῖς, ὥστε ἡμᾶς ὡς στρουθίον ἐμπαίζεσθαι " παρὰ τῶν θελόντων δουλεύειν τῷ Κυρίῳ, καὶ χατα- πατεῖσθαι ὑπὸ τῶν πνευματοφόρων ἀνδρῶν. ᾿Αλλ᾽ εἰ καὶ ἄτονο: γεγόναμεν παρ᾽ αὐτοῦ, οὐκ ἀργοῦμεν "τῆς ἐνεργείας ἡμῶν, ἀλλ᾽ οὔτε ἡσυχάζομεν τοῦ ἀντικεῖσθαι ὑμῖν, διότι ἡ φύσις ἡμῶν ἀνπνός ἐστιν. Σπείρομεν᾽ οὖν τὴν ἰδίαν ἡμῶν' κακίαν εἰς τὴν ψυχὴν ᾿ τοῦ ἀνταγωνιστοῦ, μάλιστα ἐὰν ἴδωμεν ὅτι ὑποδέχεται". πλέον ὑπεχκαίομεν" αὐτῷ τὰς ἐπιθυμίας, χαὶ λοιπὸν ὡς δυνατοὶ καὶ παλαμναῖοι ἐπιδαίνομεν αὐτῷ" ἐὰν δὲ μὴ θελήσῃ" ὑποδέξασθαι ἡμῶν τὸν σπόρον, μνηδὲ τὰ παρ᾽ ἡμῶν ὑποδαλλόμενα αὐτῷ ἡδέως χαταδέξασθαι τῇ πρὸς Θεὸν πίστει αὐτοῦ καὶ τῷ " νηφαλέῳ τοῦ νοὸς αὐτοῦ '", ὡς κάπνος εἰς ἀέρα διαλυό- μένος οὕτως ἐσόμεθα αὐτῷ '". Διὰ τοῦτο πρὸς πάντας οὐ δύναμαι '" πολεμῆσαι" διὰ τὸ μὴ πάντας τὴν τελειότητα ἔχειν. Ἐΐ γὰρ συνεχωρούμην πρὸς πάντας πολεμῆσαι, πολλοὺς ἐπερει- δομένους τῇ σῇ σκέπη "" ἐξηπάτησα ἄν. » Τότε ὁ μακαρίος ἐνεφύσησεν αὐτῇ, εἰπών᾽ « Ὦ τῆς ἀχοιμήτου ὑμῶν κακίας" οὐ παύεσθε '' γὰρ κατὰ τοῦ γένους '" τῶν ἀνθρώπων μαινόμενοι, ἕως ἂν ἡ θεία καὶ ἄχραντος χάρις" τοῦ Θεοῦ ἀναλώσῃ '΄ ὑμᾶς. » " Ταῦτα εἰπὼν αὐτῇ "ὁ ἅγιος γέρων Παχούμιος ἐπέτρεψεν ἀπελθεῖν αὐτὴν ἐπιτιμήσας καὶ παραγγείλας αὐτῇ μηδ᾽ ὅλως ἐγγίσαι τῇ μονῇ αὐτοῦ.
men?" She replied to him and said: "I have already told you that since the appearance on earth of the all-powerful force of the Savior Christ, we have been weakened to the point of being despised and ridiculed like a sparrow by men such as these, pneumatophores and who want to serve the Lord. Even if we are defeated by one (man), we do not cease (to do) our work, as much as we can, and to rise against you; we pour our wickedness into the soul of the one who resists us, especially if we see that he accepts, or at least that he lets us attack him; we inflame his passions more, then like powerful ones and (like) strong and formidable demons, we enter into him for his defeat. If he does not want to receive our seed nor accept with joy what we offer him, because of his faith in God and the prudence of his spirit, we become for him like a smoke that resolves into the air. I cannot fight them all, because not all possess perfection. If * one let me fight against you all, I would deceive many of those who rest at your side." The blessed one said to him: "How tireless is your wickedness! you do not cease to rage against the human race until the moment when the divine and pure grace descends from heaven and makes you vanish!" When he had said that, he ordered her to go where she had to go and not to set foot in his monastery anymore.


[p. 470] {#po.4.p470}

44 Of the gift of tongues that he received. - When * the blessed one was visiting the brothers in the cells and correcting the thoughts of each of them, it happened that he entered near a Roman who was of high rank and knew the Greek language well. When the Great one came near him to teach him what could be useful to him and to know the movements of his heart, the blessed one was speaking Egyptian and the brother did not know * what the Great one was saying to him; likewise the Great one did not know what the Roman was saying to him, because the blessed one did not know how to speak Greek. The Great one was therefore obliged to call a brother who could interpret what both would say. When the brother came to serve as interpreter, the Roman did not want to reveal to the Great one the faults of his heart through the intermediary of another, and said to him: "I want you alone, after God, to know the wickedness of my heart: I do not want to tell them to you through the intermediary of another, nor that another man than you hear them." 45 "Lord strong and all-powerful, if I cannot be useful to these men whom you send near me from the ends of the earth, because I do not know their language, what necessity is there that they come? but if you want them to be saved here * by my hands, give me, Lord all-powerful," for the correction of their souls, to know their language." When he had prayed for three hours and begged God much on this subject, he finished his prayer and immediately a written note of paper was sent from heaven into his right hand and, after having read it, he immediately knew the language of all tongues. He gave glory to the Father, to the Son and to the Holy Spirit, returned with great joy near this brother, and began to speak to him in Greek and in Latin without fault, to the point that this brother, hearing him, said about the Great one: "He surpasses by his

46 On a holy man named Jonas who was the gardener of one of the monasteries, and on a wonder that the great Pachomius performed in the monastery of Jonas. - The following day, the blessed one went to visit the other monasteries; he arrived at the monastery called Pbow and entered it. There was, in the middle of this monastery, a large fig tree where some children were accustomed to climb secretly, to pluck (figs), and to eat them. When the Great one entered and approached this fig tree, he saw an unclean spirit sitting there; he knew immediately that it was the demon of gluttony. When the saint recognized that it was this demon who was deceiving the children, he called the gardener and said to him: "Brother, cut down this fig tree, for it is a cause of scandal for those whose spirit is not strengthened, and it is not right that it should remain in the middle of the monastery." At these words, the gardener was very distressed.


[p. 473] {#po.4.p473}

Καὶ οἱ ἀδελφοὶ πάντες διε- θεδαιοῦντο περὶ αὐτοῦ λέγοντες ὅτι οὔτε"' τὸν οἶκον τοῦ νοσοχομείου "ὁ ἔδει ὁποῖος ἦν,
47 This gardener was named Jonas; he had spent eighty-five years in this monastery and led a pure and grave life there. He was the only one to look after the fruits, and he had planted all the trees of this monastery himself; but he never tasted any fruit until his death, not even one, while the brothers, the strangers, and those who lived in the surroundings ate them to satiety in their season. This brother wore the following garment: he had joined three skins together to cover his whole body, and that sufficed for him. He never covered himself with a blanket during the winter or with anything else during the summer: he did not know what it was to rest his body from continuous labors, for he worked constantly with a spirit full of ardor. He never tasted cooked food, neither lentils nor other food (of this kind), but only a mixture of herbs with vinegar throughout his life. The brothers affirmed of him and said: "He does not know the infirmary and knows neither where it is nor what the sick eat."
οὐδὲ τί "5 ἐσθίουσιν οἱ χαχούμενοι ἀδελφοί. Πρὸς δὲ τούτοις πᾶσιν" οὐδέποτε ἐπὶ τὸν νῶτον ἐκοιμήθη "ἡ ἕως τῆς τελευτῆς" αὐτοῦ, ἀλλ᾽ ἡμέρας μὲν ἠργάζετο" εἰς τοὺς κήπους,
Furthermore, ‘as we have heard it told about him’, never, until his death, did he lie on his back: he worked during the day in the garden, then, towards sunset,

Οὐχ ἅπτων δὲ φῶς ἔπλεχεν τὰ σχοινία ᾽, ἀλλ᾽ ἐν τῇ " σχοτίᾳ καθήμενος χαὶ ἀποστηθίζων τὰς γραφάς. Καὶ ἄλλα δὲ πολλὰ ἦν τὰ ἐπαίνων ἄξια, ἃ ἐποίει ὁ μακάριος γέρων ἐχεῖνος, ἅπερ οὐ κατετάξαμεν τῇδε τῇ διηγήσει", ἵνα μὴ εἰς μῆκος ἄπειρον τὸν λόγον ἐχτείνωμεν '".
he would take some food and enter his cell; he would sit on a seat in the middle of his cell and braid ropes until the night office. In this way, if it happened that he took a little sleep, necessitated (ananke) by his ‘bodily’ nature, he would sleep standing up and keep in his hand the ropes he was braiding. He did not light a lamp to braid the ropes, but (did so) sitting in the darkness while reciting the Scriptures by heart. He had only one linen tunic without sleeves (chiton) which he put on when he went to receive the holy and divine mysteries of Christ; then he would immediately take off and put away this sleeveless tunic, which he kept clean; it sufficed for him for those eighty-five years. This blessed old man performed many other works worthy of praise that we do not write in the drafting of this history, so that our discourse does not reach an endless length and that we do not give trouble to the faithful in the accounts they will read.
